### Throwbacks

- All MLB Teams will wear their throwback caps during the [National Baseball Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Baseball_Hall_of_Fame) induction weekend from July 25 to 27.

### Other uniforms

- The [Dodgers](18213.md) wore gold-trimmed home uniforms on March 27 and 28 to commemorate their [2024 World Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_World_Series) championship.

- All players, managers, and umpires wore #42 on April 15, the 78th anniversary of [Jackie Robinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jackie_Robinson)'s debut in the majors.

- The [Braves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlanta_Braves) and [Reds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cincinnati_Reds) wore racing themed uniforms for the [MLB Speedway Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MLB_Speedway_Classic) at [Bristol Motor Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bristol_Motor_Speedway) on August 2 and 3.

- All Cubs players wore #23 on August 2 to honor Ryne Sandberg.

- The Cubs and Pirates wore #21 on September 15 to honor [Roberto Clemente](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Clemente).

## Venues

On November 16, 2023, MLB owners approved the [Athletics' relocation from Oakland to Las Vegas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oakland_Athletics_relocation_to_Las_Vegas). The Athletics then announced on April 4, 2024, that the team would temporarily play at [Sutter Health Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sutter_Health_Park) in [West Sacramento, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Sacramento,_California), from 2025 to 2027, with an option for 2028, while their new [ballpark in Las Vegas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Las_Vegas_Stadium) is under construction.[^cite_note-Athletics-2][^cite_note-KLAS20230615-188]

Due to severe roof damage sustained at [Tropicana Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropicana_Field) caused by [Hurricane Milton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Milton), the [Tampa Bay Rays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tampa_Bay_Rays) will play at [George M. Steinbrenner Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_M._Steinbrenner_Field), the [New York Yankees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Yankees)' [spring training](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spring_training) stadium in Tampa.[^cite_note-189]

Guaranteed Rate Field, home of the [Chicago White Sox](5945.md), was renamed to [Rate Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rate_Field) during the offseason on December 17, 2024, as part of a rebrand of [Guaranteed Rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guaranteed_Rate).[^cite_note-190]

Minute Maid Park, home of the [Houston Astros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houston_Astros), was renamed to [Daikin Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daikin_Park) during the offseason on January 1, after Japanese air conditioning manufacturer [Daikin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daikin) bought the naming rights.[^cite_note-191]

## Media

### Television

#### National

This was the fourth year of the existing seven-year deals with [Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_League_Baseball_on_Fox), [TBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_League_Baseball_on_TBS), and [Apple TV+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_TV+);[^cite_note-Forbes20220309-192] and the second year of a multi-year deal with [TelevisaUnivision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Univision_Deportes) for Spanish-language broadcasts.[^cite_note-univision-193]

This was also the fourth year of a deal with [ESPN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_League_Baseball_on_ESPN). However, ESPN and Major League Baseball announced prior to the season that both parties had exercised a mutual opt-out to end the agreement following the 2025 season.[^cite_note-194] In addition, this was originally intended to be second year of an existing three-year deal with [The Roku Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Roku_Channel),[^cite_note-195] but that agreement was also terminated after the 2025 season.[^cite_note-196]

##### Linear television

- Fox continued to air their *[Fox Saturday Baseball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Saturday_Baseball)* and *[Baseball Night in America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baseball_Night_in_America)* slate of games on most Saturdays during the season and select Thursday nights (these games will be in regional windows with at least two games per night). Fox exclusively aired the [MLB Speedway Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MLB_Speedway_Classic) as part of their package. Fox and FS1 split coverage of the [MLB Tokyo Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Major_League_Baseball_games_played_outside_the_United_States_and_Canada), with Fox aired the first game and FS1 airing the second; although Fox aired the first game, MLB allowed the Cubs' [Marquee Sports Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marquee_Sports_Network) and the Dodgers' [Spectrum SportsNet LA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spectrum_SportsNet_LA) to produce their own broadcasts in the [Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_metropolitan_area) and [Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Los_Angeles) metropolitan areas.

  [^cite_note-197]

  &#x20;Fox will air the [2025 MLB All-Star Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Major_League_Baseball_All-Star_Game). [FS1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MLB_on_FS1) will also broadcast games, mainly on Saturday afternoons, which will be both non-exclusive and co-exist with regional broadcasts.

  [^cite_note-Fox_rights-198]

- TBS will continue to broadcast *[MLB Tuesday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MLB_Tuesday)*. Most games are blacked out in the home markets of the teams playing, however, TBS is allowed to co-exist once with a teams' local broadcast.

  [^cite_note-TBS_rights-199]

  &#x20;TBS also brought in [Alex Faust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Faust) as the secondary play-by-play voice on both *MLB Tuesday* and in the [Division Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Division_Series), replacing [Bob Costas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Costas).

  [^cite_note-200]

- ESPN continued to broadcast *[Sunday Night Baseball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunday_Night_Baseball)*, with the option to show alternate broadcasts, along with at least five other exclusive regular-season games. For the first time since [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Major_League_Baseball_season), ESPN aired a doubleheader on [Opening Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opening_Day).

  [^cite_note-201]

  &#x20;ESPN will also air a doubleheader on Sunday, July 6, as part of [July 4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Day_\(United_States\)) weekend.

  [^cite_note-202]

  &#x20;The network also has the rights to the [Home Run Derby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Home_Run_Derby).

  [^cite_note-ESPN_rights-203]

  &#x20;Select games may air on [ABC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_League_Baseball_on_ABC) or [ESPN2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN2). [Joe Buck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Buck), who was the lead MLB on Fox play-by-play commentator until he moved to ESPN for [Monday Night Football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monday_Night_Football) in 2022, called his first nationally televised baseball game for ESPN on Opening Day in 2025.

  [^cite_note-204]

- [MLB Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MLB_Network) continued to broadcast a regular slate of games. While most games were simulcasts of the teams' local broadcast, select games were produced by the network under its *[MLB Network Showcase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MLB_Network_Showcase)* banner. All games were blacked out in the home markets of the teams playing.

  [^cite_note-205]

- [UniMás](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UniM%C3%A1s) and [TUDN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TUDN_\(American_TV_channel\)) aired the weekly whiparound show *MLB En Vivo* (lit. 'MLB Live') on Tuesday nights throughout the season, the weekly studio show *MLB Esta Semana* (lit. 'MLB This Week') on Saturday nights, and postseason coverage.

  [^cite_note-\:1-206]

##### Streaming

- For the first time, [ESPN+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN+) and [Disney+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney+) simulcast coverage of ESPN's Opening Day games, and the network had the option to simulcast additional ESPN-produced games.

  [^cite_note-207]

  &#x20;However, unlike previous seasons, ESPN+ did not stream a daily MLB game produced by RSN's.

  [^cite_note-208]

  &#x20;Beginning August 24, ESPN's new [direct-to-consumer service of the same name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN_\(streaming_service\)) will stream all ESPN-produced games.

  [^cite_note-209]

  [^cite_note-210]

- [HBO Max](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_\(streaming_service\)) continued to simulcast TBS' games, however beginning this season it only streamed games on its ad free tier.

  [^cite_note-211]

- [Apple TV+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_TV+) streamed *[Friday Night Baseball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friday_Night_Baseball)*, which featured two exclusive games each Friday during the season.

  [^cite_note-Forbes20220309-192]

- [The Roku Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Roku_Channel) streamed *[MLB Sunday Leadoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MLB_Sunday_Leadoff)*, which featured one game each week from May to August, with the option to air games in the early or late window.

  [^cite_note-roku-212]

- Beginning August 21, [Fox One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_One_\(streaming_service\)) simulcast in market and national Fox games, along with all FS1 games.

  [^cite_note-213]

  [^cite_note-214]

- [MLB.tv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MLB.tv) continued to stream out-of-market games (including out-of-market Fox games) and all Roku and MLB Network games.

  [^cite_note-roku-212]

  [^cite_note-215]

##### Postseason

During the postseason, the ESPN networks (including ABC, with Spanish–language simulcasts on ESPN Deportes) aired all four Wild Card Series.[^cite_note-ESPN_rights-203] TNT Sports (TBS and truTV) then broadcast the National League Division Series and the National League Championship Series,[^cite_note-TBS_rights-199] and Fox Sports (Fox and FS1, with Spanish-language simulcasts on Fox Deportes) broadcast the American League Division Series, the American League Championship Series and the World Series.[^cite_note-Fox_rights-198]

#### Local

See also: [Diamond Sports Group § Bankruptcy (2023–25)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diamond_Sports_Group#Bankruptcy_\(2023%E2%80%9325\))

- The Chicago White Sox, the [NBA](22093.md)'s [Chicago Bulls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Bulls), the [NHL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NHL)'s [Chicago Blackhawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Blackhawks), and [Standard Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_Media) launched the [Chicago Sports Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Sports_Network) in October 2024, replacing [NBC Sports Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC_Sports_Chicago) as their regional broadcaster. The new network is carried via both broadcast and subscription television.

  [^cite_note-216]

  [^cite_note-217]

  &#x20;In June, Chicago Sports Network ended its broadcast television carriage in Illinois after reaching a carriage agreement with the subscription television service [Comcast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comcast). This did not affect the network's broadcast carriage in Iowa, Indiana, Kentucky, and Michigan.

  [^cite_note-218]

  &#x20;However, in July, Chicago Sports Network announced an agreement with [WCIU-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WCIU-TV) in Chicago to simulcast seven White Sox games on broadcast television.

  [^cite_note-219]

- [MLB Advanced Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MLB_Advanced_Media) reached agreements with the Athletics, Dodgers, Giants, Mets, and Phillies to launch in-market [direct-to-consumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct-to-consumer) (DTC) streaming packages via [MLB.tv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MLB.tv). For all teams except the Dodgers, subscribers will have the ability to bundle the service with MLB.tv's out-of-market package.

  [^cite_note-220]

  [^cite_note-221]

  [^cite_note-222]

- *[Sportico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sportico)* reported that [NBC Sports California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC_Sports_California) had renegotiated its agreement with the Athletics to account for its transitional relocation to Sacramento. The network's broadcast territory has historically included the Sacramento market, where it serves as the regional broadcaster of the [NBA](22093.md)'s [Sacramento Kings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacramento_Kings).

  [^cite_note-223]

- Amid the now-resolved bankruptcy of [Main Street Sports Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Main_Street_Sports_Group) (formerly Diamond Sports Group)—owners of the [FanDuel Sports Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FanDuel_Sports_Network) (FDSN, formerly Bally Sports) regional sports networks,

  [^cite_note-exit-224]

  &#x20;its contracts with the Cleveland Guardians, Milwaukee Brewers, Minnesota Twins, and Texas Rangers expired at the end of the 2024 season,

  [^cite_note-225]

  [^cite_note-\:102-226]

  [^cite_note-227]

  &#x20;while the Reds announced an agreement to opt out of its existing contract with Diamond.

  [^cite_note-\:3-228]

  - [MLB Local Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MLB_Local_Media) assumed the rights to the Guardians, Twins, and Mariners beginning in the 2025 season.

    [^cite_note-229]

    [^cite_note-230]

    &#x20;MLB-produced Mariners telecasts continued to be distributed by [Root Sports Northwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Root_Sports_Northwest) and its newly-launched DTC service Root Sports Stream;

    [^cite_note-231]

    [^cite_note-232]

    &#x20;on September 27, 2025, the Mariners announced that Root Sports Northwest would close after the conclusion of the season, and that MLB Local Media would take over distribution of the team's games beginning in 2026.

    [^cite_note-233]

  - The Brewers and Reds were originally announced as also moving to MLB Local Media, but Diamond later re-entered into negotiations with both teams, and reached new agreements for them to remain on [FanDuel Sports Network Wisconsin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FanDuel_Sports_Network_Wisconsin) and [Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FanDuel_Sports_Network_Ohio) respectively. The new contracts also included additional options to carry the teams' games on DTC platforms.

    [^cite_note-\:4-234]

    [^cite_note-\:3-228]

  - The Rangers launched the in-house [Rangers Sports Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rangers_Sports_Network) (RSN); it operates similarly to MLB Local Media, distributing games via agreements with television providers within the team's market, and offering a DTC service hosted by [Victory+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victory+) (which is available via a paid subscription, or at no additional charge to [authenticated subscribers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV_Everywhere) of television providers who carry Rangers games).

    [^cite_note-\:0-235]

    [^cite_note-236]

    [^cite_note-\:2-237]

    &#x20;The Rangers also announced agreements with [Gray Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gray_Media) and [Nexstar Media Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nexstar_Media_Group) to syndicate a package of 15 games—primarily Friday home games—to a statewide network of [broadcast television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrestrial_television) stations, with [KDAF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KDAF) in Dallas as flagship station.

    [^cite_note-\:2-237]

    [^cite_note-238]

  - Diamond renegotiated its contracts with most of the remaining teams it televises.

    [^cite_note-reds-239]

    [^cite_note-240]

    [^cite_note-\:4-234]

    [^cite_note-\:3-228]

- On November 22, 2024, the Miami Marlins parted ways with television play-by-play announcer [Paul Severino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Severino) after seven seasons.

  [^cite_note-241]

  &#x20;On January 17, 2025, the Marlins announced that they have moved announcer Kyle Sielaff from radio to television play-by-play.

  [^cite_note-242]

- Teams with local media agreements with [FanDuel Sports Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FanDuel_Sports_Network) and [MLB Local Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MLB_Local_Media) began simulcasting a limited number of games on [over-the-air stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrestrial_television). These deals resembled arrangements made under some of FDSN's [NBA](22093.md) and [NHL](21809.md) contracts, which similarly enable teams to simulcast a limited package of games on broadcast television.

  [^cite_note-243]

  - In addition to airing on FanDuel Sports Network, the Braves, Cardinals, Royals, and Reds announced agreements to simulcast packages of games on networks of Gray Media broadcast stations. The Braves aired a package of 15 regular-season games in simulcast with [FanDuel Sports Network South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FanDuel_Sports_Network_South), with [WPCH-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WPCH-TV) in Atlanta as the flagship station. In addition, Gray exclusively produced and aired 10 spring training games.

    [^cite_note-244]

    &#x20;The Cardinals aired a package of at-least 10 regular season games in simulcast with [FanDuel Sports Network Midwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FanDuel_Sports_Network_Midwest), with [Matrix Midwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matrix_Midwest) as the flagship station.

    [^cite_note-245]

    &#x20;The Royals aired a package of 10 regular-season games in simulcast with [FanDuel Sports Network Kansas City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FanDuel_Sports_Network_Kansas_City), with [KSMO-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KSMO-TV) as the flagship station.

    [^cite_note-246]

    &#x20;The Reds aired a package of 10 regular season games in simulcast with [FanDuel Sports Network Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FanDuel_Sports_Network_Ohio), with [WXIX-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WXIX-TV) and [Rock Entertainment Sports Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_Entertainment_Sports_Network) as the flagship stations.

    [^cite_note-247]

  - In March 2025, the Brewers and Tigers reached agreements with [Fox Television Stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Television_Stations) (a former sister to FDSN) to serve as flagships for similar arrangements, with Detroit's [WJBK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WJBK) to simulcast 10 Tigers games with [FanDuel Sports Network Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FanDuel_Sports_Network_Detroit),

    [^cite_note-248]

    &#x20;and [WITI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WITI_\(TV\)) in Milwaukee to simulcast 10 regular season Brewers games and 3 spring training games with [FanDuel Sports Network Wisconsin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FanDuel_Sports_Network_Wisconsin). The Brewers' radio rightsholder [Good Karma Brands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Good_Karma_Brands) is handling syndication agreements for the Brewers' package, which includes affiliates in other Wisconsin markets.

    [^cite_note-249]

    &#x20;In May, the Angels reached an agreement with [KCOP-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KCOP-TV) to simulcast 12 games.

    [^cite_note-250]

  - Later in March, the Diamondbacks, Padres, and Rockies, whose television rights are controlled by MLB Local Media, reached similar agreements with [Tegna Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tegna_Inc.) to simulcast 10 games. The flagship stations would be [KFMB-DT2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KFMB-DT2) (The CW San Diego) for the Padres, [KTVD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KTVD) for the Rockies, and [KPNX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KPNX) for the Diamondbacks.

    [^cite_note-251]

    &#x20;The Guardians reached a similar agreement with Tegna in April, with [WKYC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WKYC) serving as the Guardians' flagship.

    [^cite_note-252]

    &#x20;In April, the Twins also announced a 10-game simulcast agreement with Fox Television Stations. [KMSP-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KMSP-TV) served as the flagship station for these games; Gray Media distributed games in other markets.

    [^cite_note-253]

  - In April, the Marlins and Rays reached agreements with [CBS News and Stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS_News_and_Stations) to simulcast 15 games. The Marlins' flagship was [WBFS-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WBFS-TV), while the Rays' flagship was [WTOG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WTOG).

    [^cite_note-254]

    [^cite_note-255]

- On February 21, 2025, the Seattle Mariners promoted announcer [Aaron Goldsmith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aaron_Goldsmith) to lead television play-by-play following the departure of [Dave Sims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dave_Sims) to the New York Yankees' radio booth. The Mariners also parted ways with long-time television analyst [Mike Blowers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Blowers) after a 17-year tenure. As a result, analyst duties were split between [Jay Buhner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jay_Buhner), [Dave Valle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dave_Valle), [Angie Mentink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angie_Mentink), and [Ryan Rowland-Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryan_Rowland-Smith).

  [^cite_note-256]

  [^cite_note-257]

- On March 3, the Orioles and Nationals reached a settlement to end the teams' dispute over television rights fee payments. [Mid-Atlantic Sports Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MASN) (MASN), which is owned and operated by the Orioles but airs both teams' games, pays rights fees to the Nationals. Through the settlement, the Nationals' agreement with the Orioles and MASN would end following the 2025 season.

  [^cite_note-258]

- On March 12, the Braves announced that they would introduce Spanish-language commentary on [SAP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_audio_program) for regional games on FanDuel Sports Network, with [Francisco X. Rivera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_X._Rivera) on play-by-play.

  [^cite_note-259]

- On March 18, the [NBC Sports Regional Networks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC_Sports_Regional_Networks)—the broadcast home of the Athletics, Giants and Phillies—launched in-market DTC services as paid add-ons for [NBCUniversal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBCUniversal)'s streaming service [Peacock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peacock_\(streaming_service\)), which include all programming broadcast by the networks. Access to these services require an active Peacock subscription.

  [^cite_note-260]

  &#x20;The next day, MLB announced separate agreements with the teams to also offer DTC packages via MLB.tv; these packages do not require a Peacock subscription, but does not include other programming from the teams' networks.

  [^cite_note-261]

- On March 24, Nationals TV play-by-play announcer [Bob Carpenter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Carpenter_\(sportscaster\)) announced that he will retire following the conclusion of the 2025 season. Carpenter has called Nationals games since the [2006 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Washington_Nationals_season), and previously called games for the Texas Rangers and St. Louis Cardinals, as well as occasional national assignments with ESPN.

  [^cite_note-262]

- On April 21, [Mid-Atlantic Sports Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MASN), the home of the Nationals and Orioles, announced the launch of a direct-to-consumer streaming service. The Astros are now the only MLB team without a DTC option.

  [^cite_note-263]

- In May, the Athletics announced an agreement with [Gray Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gray_Media) to simulcast 15 games in Las Vegas, in preparation for the teams' move to the city, on [KVVU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KVVU) and [Silver State Sports & Entertainment Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silver_State_Sports_&_Entertainment_Network).

  [^cite_note-264]

#### International

- ESPN will additionally air its slate of games in Latin America, sub-Saharan Africa, Oceania and the Netherlands, and will air games through [Disney+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney+) in select markets in Asia and Europe.

  [^cite_note-\:5-265]

- TNT Sports will additionally air its slate of games in Puerto Rico, the United Kingdom, and Ireland.

  [^cite_note-\:5-265]

- Fox Sports will additionally air its slate of games in Argentina, Mexico, and Puerto Rico.

  [^cite_note-\:5-265]

- MLB Network International will air select games worldwide.

### Radio

#### National

- This will be the final season that [ESPN Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN_Radio) will be airing games (due to ESPN opting out of this deal along with its TV deal), which includes Sunday night baseball, the entire All Star Weekend and the entire MLB Postseason.

  [^cite_note-266]

- This is the second season of the league's five-year deal with [SiriusXM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SiriusXM) and [SiriusXM Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SiriusXM_Canada) to simulcast all 30 teams' local regular season and postseason broadcasts in English and Spanish.

  [^cite_note-267]

#### Local

- On November 14, 2024, the New York Yankees announced the hiring of [Dave Sims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dave_Sims) as the team's primary radio play-by-play announcer, succeeding [John Sterling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Sterling_\(sportscaster\)). Sims was previously the lead television play-by-play announcer of the Seattle Mariners, holding the job since 2007.

  [^cite_note-268]

- On November 15, the Los Angeles Dodgers announced the hiring of [Luis Cruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Cruz) (who played for the team in [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Los_Angeles_Dodgers_season) and [2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Los_Angeles_Dodgers_season)) as an analyst for its Spanish-language radio broadcasts, succeeding [Fernando Valenzuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Valenzuela)—who had died on October 22, 2024.

  [^cite_note-269]

- On January 16, 2025, the Milwaukee Brewers' long-time play-by-play announcer [Bob Uecker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Uecker) died at the age of 90. A [Ford C. Frick Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ford_C._Frick_Award) winner in 2003, Uecker had been the team's primary radio announcer since 1971, and also worked national broadcasts for [ABC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_League_Baseball_on_ABC) and [NBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_League_Baseball_on_NBC).

  [^cite_note-270]

  &#x20;The Brewers will utilize [Jeff Levering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Levering), Lane Grindle, and Josh Maurer on radio play-by-play during the season, with Grindle or Maurer calling games when Levering is on television.

  [^cite_note-271]

- The Boston Red Sox promoted [Will Flemming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Will_Flemming) to lead radio play-by-play following the retirement of [Joe Castiglione](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Castiglione). Flemming is the brother of San Francisco Giants announcer [Dave Flemming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dave_Flemming).

  [^cite_note-272]

## Retired numbers

- [David Wright](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Wright) had his No. 5 retired by the [New York Mets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Mets) on July 19. This was the tenth number retired by the team.

  [^cite_note-323]

- [Ichiro Suzuki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ichiro_Suzuki) had his No. 51 retired by the [Seattle Mariners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Mariners) on August 9. This was the third number retired by the team.

  [^cite_note-324]

- [Billy Wagner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billy_Wagner) had his No. 13 retired by the [Houston Astros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houston_Astros) on August 16. This was the tenth number retired by the team.

  [^cite_note-325]
